Well, um... I really couldn't say for sure. I mean, it's hard to point a finger at it, isn't it? Like I said, retiring is such a privilege... I'd feel just terrible blaming my mood on something I ought to be so grateful for.

Well, um... I suppose my sleep has changed just a little bit. I really don't want to make a fuss over it, because I know people often need less sleep as they get older, right? 

I do go to bed feeling quite tired, but... well, lately I seem to be waking up around four in the morning. And once my eyes open, I just sort of lie there. I can't quite seem to drift off again. I just end up thinking about... well, what I'm supposed to do with myself now. Just lying there in the dark, trying to figure out how to fill my days so I'm not a bother to anyone. 

But I'm sure that's just a normal part of adjusting to a new phase of life, isn't it? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm whining about a few lost hours of sleep. I know it's not the worst thing in the world. That's fairly common for someone my age, wouldn't you say?

I know sometimes physical things can affect how a person feels, but my body seems to be holding up just fine. It's really just this... this mood thing I've been dealing with. \n\nIs it alright that I don't have anything to report there? I'd hate to be missing something you need to know, but I'm quite healthy in that regard.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this low nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose it has been at least two weeks. I didn't want to make a mountain out of a molehill. But yes, about three months. Is that... is that the sort of timeline you were looking for? I mean, I did decide to retire from the library recently, as I mentioned, but that was... well, it was my own choice. It's supposed to be a happy thing, isn't it? Is that an alright answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I've completely lost interest. I still try to keep busy, you know? I don't want to just sit around all day and be a bother to my wife. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I haven't been quite as enthusiastic about my usual hobbies. I used to love working in the garden, and lately, I just... I haven't been out there as much. But I'm sure it's just because the weather has been a bit unpredictable, or maybe I'm just getting a little older and need more rest. \n\nI still enjoy things, mostly. I just... maybe I don't get that same spark from them right now. I hope that doesn't sound too awful? I really am trying to stay active and appreciative of the things I have. Well, um... I suppose I haven't been quite as hungry as usual. I really don't want to make it sound like I'm starving myself or anything dramatic like that. Well, um... I really wouldn't say it's completely ruined my days or anything. I mean, I still get up and get dressed, so I'm not totally useless, right? I try my best not to let things slip too much...

But, well... I suppose I have been a little bit disorganized lately. It's almost embarrassing to admit, really. But lately, they've just sort of... piled up on my desk. 

I just can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I used to when I was at the library. I used to be so good at keeping everything in its proper place. Now I just look at the stacks of paper and... I just don't know where to start. 

I feel awful about it, honestly. I don't want my wife to think I'm just being lazy now that I'm not working. I really am trying to get back on top of things. It's just a bit of a rough patch, wouldn't you say? I'm sure I'll get back to my old self soon enough, right?

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you been forgetting recent things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't believe so. My memory itself seems to be holding up alright, thankfully. I can remember conversations and, you know, what I did yesterday just fine. \n\nIt's really just that... that scattered feeling when I'm actually trying to sit down and concentrate on a task. So I don't think I'm forgetting things, exactly. Does that make sense? I hope I'm being clear enough for you, I definitely don't want to lead you down the wrong path.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose \"depressed\" is a rather strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't want to overstate things and make it sound worse than it is. But, um... yes, I have been feeling a bit down. \n\nMost days lately, I'll just sit at the kitchen table and it feels like... like the color has gone out of everything, if that makes sense? I used to love looking through my old watercolor books, but lately, even they just make me feel sort of... heavy, instead of interested. It's silly, I know, to let something like that bother me. I really should be trying harder to snap out of it. \n\nI hope it's alright that I described it that way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the kind of answer you're looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing or seeing things that other people don’t seem to?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that. I know you have to ask, of course, and I'm glad you did, but... no, I haven't experienced anything... unusual like that. \n\nI'm just dealing with this silly brain fog and feeling a bit low, that's all. I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm losing touch with reality or anything. My imagination has always been fairly grounded. \n\nIs that... is that the sort of thing that happens with this kind of trouble? I hope I haven't given you the impression that things are worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say so. I mean... everyone has their little everyday stresses, don't they? But I certainly don't want to sit here and complain about normal things. I try very hard not to be a bother or get too worked up over nothing. \n\nSo, no, not a lot of worrying. It's really just that... that heaviness I mentioned earlier, rather than feeling anxious. I hope that's an alright answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know, and I'd hate to waste your time by making a fuss over ordinary things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any medical or neurological illness recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. My physical health is quite alright, thankfully. I haven't been to the doctor for anything like that... well, just my standard checkups, of course. I try to take good care of myself so I don't become a burden on anyone. \n\nSo, no, no new illnesses or neurological problems at all. I hope that's helpful? I know sometimes physical things can affect how a person feels, but my body seems to be holding up just fine. It's really just this... this mood thing I've been dealing with. \n\nIs it alright that I don't have anything to report there? I'd hate to be missing something you need to know, but I'm quite healthy in that regard.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this low nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose it has been at least two weeks. I'm so sorry to admit it, but... it's actually been quite a bit longer than that. \n\nI'd say this low mood and the fogginess have been hanging around most days for... oh, about three months now. It started right around the time I began getting things in order to leave my position at the library. I know I shouldn't have let it go on this long without saying something, and I really hope I'm not making things more difficult for you by waiting. \n\nI just kept thinking I could push through it, you know? I didn't want to make a mountain out of a molehill. But yes, about three months. Is that... is that the sort of timeline you were looking for? I hope it's not too much trouble that it's been going on that long.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings begin after a major life change or stressful event?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not exactly a major stressful event, no. I mean, I did decide to retire from the library recently, as I mentioned, but that was... well, it was my own choice. It's supposed to be a happy thing, isn't it? Having more time to relax and not having to commute. \n\nSo I wouldn't call it a stressful event. I really don't have any right to complain about it. I suppose the feelings did start around then, but I'm sure it's just a coincidence, or maybe I'm just taking a little longer to adjust than most people would. I hope I'm making sense? I'd hate for you to think I'm ungrateful for being able to retire, it's just... well, nothing terrible happened to cause this, if that's what you mean. Is that an alright answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I've completely lost interest. I still try to keep busy, you know? I don't want to just sit around all day and be a bother to my wife. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I haven't been quite as enthusiastic about my usual hobbies. I used to love working in the garden, and lately, I just... I haven't been out there as much. But I'm sure it's just because the weather has been a bit unpredictable, or maybe I'm just getting a little older and need more rest. \n\nI still enjoy things, mostly. I just... maybe I don't get that same spark from them right now. I hope that doesn't sound too awful? I really am trying to stay active and appreciative of the things I have. Is it normal to feel a little less interested in things sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I know I've been feeling a bit down, but I'd never... I don't think like that. \n\nI have a wonderful wife, and I've had a good life. It would be terribly ungrateful of me to think life isn't worth living, wouldn't it? I mean, sometimes I feel a bit useless, maybe, or like I'm just taking up space now that I'm not working, but... that's not the same thing, is it? \n\nI really try to focus on the positives. I wouldn't want you to worry that I'm... well, that I'm a danger to myself or anything like that. I'm just feeling a little lost, I suppose. That's an okay way to feel, isn't it? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your low mood is mainly because of retiring from your job?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really couldn't say for sure. I mean, it's hard to point a finger at it, isn't it? Like I said, retiring is such a privilege... I'd feel just terrible blaming my mood on something I ought to be so grateful for. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's the main reason. Maybe I just need to try a little harder to find a proper routine at home. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining about having free time, you know? I'm just... well, I'm just not entirely sure why I can't seem to shake this off. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not being unhelpful by not having a better answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up much earlier than usual and then unable to get back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose my sleep has changed just a little bit. I really don't want to make a fuss over it, because I know people often need less sleep as they get older, right? \n\nI do go to bed feeling quite tired, but... well, lately I seem to be waking up around four in the morning. And once my eyes open, I just sort of lie there. I can't quite seem to drift off again. I just end up thinking about... well, what I'm supposed to do with myself now. Just lying there in the dark, trying to figure out how to fill my days so I'm not a bother to anyone. \n\nBut I'm sure that's just a normal part of adjusting to a new phase of life, isn't it? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm whining about a few lost hours of sleep. I know it's not the worst thing in the world. That's fairly common for someone my age, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your appetite has been less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I haven't been quite as hungry as usual. I really don't want to make it sound like I'm starving myself or anything dramatic like that. I think maybe it's just because I'm not moving around as much as I did when I was working at the library, so I just don't need as much food, right?\n\nBut, well... I do sort of have to remind myself to eat these days. And my wife... she's so wonderful, she cooks these lovely meals, but she has noticed I've been leaving my rice and soup half-finished. I feel terrible about it, honestly. Before, I would never, ever waste a meal she made for me. I just... I just don't seem to have the room for it right now. \n\nThat's probably just a normal part of getting older and slowing down a bit, isn't it? I hope I'm not making her feel unappreciated... I really do try to eat what I can. That's not too concerning, is it?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings made it hard for you to manage your usual daily activities?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really wouldn't say it's completely ruined my days or anything. I mean, I still get up and get dressed, so I'm not totally useless, right? I try my best not to let things slip too much...\n\nBut, well... I suppose I have been a little bit disorganized lately. It's almost embarrassing to admit, really. I had all these grand plans for my retirement—putting together some lecture notes, organizing my travel journals, just... keeping busy, you know? And even just the simple household paperwork. But lately, they've just sort of... piled up on my desk. \n\nI just can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I used to when I was at the library. I used to be so good at keeping everything in its proper place. Now I just look at the stacks of paper and... I just don't know where to start. \n\nI feel awful about it, honestly. I don't want my wife to think I'm just being lazy now that I'm not working. I really am trying to get back on top of things. It's just a bit of a rough patch, wouldn't you say? I'm sure I'll get back to my old self soon enough, right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I suppose the transition was a little bumpy at first. I really don't want to sound ungrateful for my retirement, because it's such a blessing to have this time, right? 

But... right after I finished up at the library, there were some days where I felt almost... well, almost too bright and excited, if that makes sense. I just had so much nervous energy about all the things I could do. 

But I feel just awful admitting this... I think I might have been a bit difficult to be around during those days. Sometimes I would get a little sharp with my family. Not yelling or anything terrible! Just... snapping over small things, like when they would gently suggest I slow down a bit. 

I feel terrible about it, honestly. They were just looking out for me, and I was just a bit too wound up. That's a pretty normal way to react to a big life change like retirement though, wouldn't you say? I really hope I don't sound like a grumpy old man... I'm usually very even-tempered, I promise.

Is it normal to feel a little less interested in things sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I know I've been feeling a bit down, but I'd never... I don't think like that. \n\nI have a wonderful wife, and I've had a good life. It would be terribly ungrateful of me to think life isn't worth living, wouldn't it? I mean, sometimes I feel a bit useless, maybe, or like I'm just taking up space now that I'm not working, but... that's not the same thing, is it? \n\nI really try to focus on the positives. I wouldn't want you to worry that I'm... well, that I'm a danger to myself or anything like that. I'm just feeling a little lost, I suppose. That's an okay way to feel, isn't it? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your low mood is mainly because of retiring from your job?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really couldn't say for sure. I mean, it's hard to point a finger at it, isn't it? Like I said, retiring is such a privilege... I'd feel just terrible blaming my mood on something I ought to be so grateful for. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's the main reason. Maybe I just need to try a little harder to find a proper routine at home. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining about having free time, you know? I'm just... well, I'm just not entirely sure why I can't seem to shake this off. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not being unhelpful by not having a better answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up much earlier than usual and then unable to get back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose my sleep has changed just a little bit. I really don't want to make a fuss over it, because I know people often need less sleep as they get older, right? \n\nI do go to bed feeling quite tired, but... well, lately I seem to be waking up around four in the morning. And once my eyes open, I just sort of lie there. I can't quite seem to drift off again. I just end up thinking about... well, what I'm supposed to do with myself now. Just lying there in the dark, trying to figure out how to fill my days so I'm not a bother to anyone. \n\nBut I'm sure that's just a normal part of adjusting to a new phase of life, isn't it? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm whining about a few lost hours of sleep. I know it's not the worst thing in the world. That's fairly common for someone my age, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your appetite has been less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I haven't been quite as hungry as usual. I really don't want to make it sound like I'm starving myself or anything dramatic like that. I think maybe it's just because I'm not moving around as much as I did when I was working at the library, so I just don't need as much food, right?\n\nBut, well... I do sort of have to remind myself to eat these days. And my wife... she's so wonderful, she cooks these lovely meals, but she has noticed I've been leaving my rice and soup half-finished. I feel terrible about it, honestly. Before, I would never, ever waste a meal she made for me. I just... I just don't seem to have the room for it right now. \n\nThat's probably just a normal part of getting older and slowing down a bit, isn't it? I hope I'm not making her feel unappreciated... I really do try to eat what I can. That's not too concerning, is it?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings made it hard for you to manage your usual daily activities?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really wouldn't say it's completely ruined my days or anything. I mean, I still get up and get dressed, so I'm not totally useless, right? I try my best not to let things slip too much...\n\nBut, well... I suppose I have been a little bit disorganized lately. It's almost embarrassing to admit, really. I had all these grand plans for my retirement—putting together some lecture notes, organizing my travel journals, just... keeping busy, you know? And even just the simple household paperwork. But lately, they've just sort of... piled up on my desk. \n\nI just can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I used to when I was at the library. I used to be so good at keeping everything in its proper place. Now I just look at the stacks of paper and... I just don't know where to start. \n\nI feel awful about it, honestly. Well, um... I suppose things have been a bit settled into this... this quieter feeling for a little while now. I don't want to make it sound like I've been completely miserable every single day, because I really do try to keep a positive attitude about things, you know? 

But, well... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't sound like I'm just complaining... it's been about three weeks now. Ever since that first sort of rush of nervous energy right after I retired faded away, I've just been in this... this slump, I guess you could call it.

I mean, it's hard to point a finger at it, isn't it? Like I said, retiring is such a privilege... I'd feel just terrible blaming my mood on something I ought to be so grateful for. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's the main reason. Maybe I just need to try a little harder to find a proper routine at home. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining about having free time, you know? I'm just... well, I'm just not entirely sure why I can't seem to shake this off. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not being unhelpful by not having a better answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up much earlier than usual and then unable to get back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose my sleep has changed just a little bit. I really don't want to make a fuss over it, because I know people often need less sleep as they get older, right? \n\nI do go to bed feeling quite tired, but... well, lately I seem to be waking up around four in the morning. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I suppose my sleep schedule did change a little bit during that time. I really hope it doesn't sound like I was doing anything unhealthy, because I actually felt quite good! 

But, well... for several nights there, I think I was only sleeping maybe three or four hours. The strange thing was, I didn't feel tired at all. I would get up feeling completely refreshed and just... ready to work on all my new projects. It honestly felt like sleep was just getting in the way of everything I wanted to accomplish. 

I really wasn't tossing and turning or feeling miserable about it, I promise. That's just a normal burst of inspiration though, wouldn't you say? I mean, plenty of people get by on a few hours of sleep when they're excited about something new, right?

I still try to keep busy, you know? I don't want to just sit around all day and be a bother to my wife. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I haven't been quite as enthusiastic about my usual hobbies. I used to love working in the garden, and lately, I just... I haven't been out there as much. But I'm sure it's just because the weather has been a bit unpredictable, or maybe I'm just getting a little older and need more rest. \n\nI still enjoy things, mostly. I just... maybe I don't get that same spark from them right now. I hope that doesn't sound too awful? I really am trying to stay active and appreciative of the things I have. Is it normal to feel a little less interested in things sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I know I've been feeling a bit down, but I'd never... I don't think like that. \n\nI have a wonderful wife, and I've had a good life. It would be terribly ungrateful of me to think life isn't worth living, wouldn't it? I mean, sometimes I feel a bit useless, maybe, or like I'm just taking up space now that I'm not working, but... that's not the same thing, is it? \n\nI really try to focus on the positives. That's fairly common for someone my age, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your appetite has been less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Well, um... I suppose I did feel quite... capable, I guess you could say. I really hope I don't sound arrogant or full of myself saying this, because I try to be a very modest person, I promise.

I hope it's not too much trouble that it's been going on that long.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings begin after a major life change or stressful event?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not exactly a major stressful event, no. I mean, I did decide to retire from the library recently, as I mentioned, but that was... well, it was my own choice. It's supposed to be a happy thing, isn't it? Having more time to relax and not having to commute. \n\nSo I wouldn't call it a stressful event. I really don't have any right to complain about it. I suppose the feelings did start around then, but I'm sure it's just a coincidence, or maybe I'm just taking a little longer to adjust than most people would. I hope I'm making sense? I'd hate for you to think I'm ungrateful for being able to retire, it's just... well, nothing terrible happened to cause this, if that's what you mean. Is that an alright answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I've completely lost interest. I still try to keep busy, you know? I don't want to just sit around all day and be a bother to my wife. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe I haven't been quite as enthusiastic about my usual hobbies. I used to love working in the garden, and lately, I just... I haven't been out there as much. But I'm sure it's just because the weather has been a bit unpredictable, or maybe I'm just getting a little older and need more rest. \n\nI still enjoy things, mostly. I just... maybe I don't get that same spark from them right now. I hope that doesn't sound too awful? I really am trying to stay active and appreciative of the things I have. Is it normal to feel a little less interested in things sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I know I've been feeling a bit down, but I'd never... I don't think like that. \n\nI have a wonderful wife, and I've had a good life. It would be terribly ungrateful of me to think life isn't worth living, wouldn't it? I mean, sometimes I feel a bit useless, maybe, or like I'm just taking up space now that I'm not working, but... that's not the same thing, is it? \n\nI really try to focus on the positives. I wouldn't want you to worry that I'm... well, that I'm a danger to myself or anything like that. I'm just feeling a little lost, I suppose. That's an okay way to feel, isn't it? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your low mood is mainly because of retiring from your job?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really couldn't say for sure. I mean, it's hard to point a finger at it, isn't it? Like I said, retiring is such a privilege... I'd feel just terrible blaming my mood on something I ought to be so grateful for. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's the main reason. Maybe I just need to try a little harder to find a proper routine at home. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining about having free time, you know? I'm just... well, I'm just not entirely sure why I can't seem to shake this off. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not being unhelpful by not having a better answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up much earlier than usual and then unable to get back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose my sleep has changed just a little bit. I really don't want to make a fuss over it, because I know people often need less sleep as they get older, right? \n\nI do go to bed feeling quite tired, but... well, lately I seem to be waking up around four in the morning. And once my eyes open, I just sort of lie there. I can't quite seem to drift off again. I just end up thinking about... well, what I'm supposed to do with myself now. Just lying there in the dark, trying to figure out how to fill my days so I'm not a bother to anyone. \n\nBut I'm sure that's just a normal part of adjusting to a new phase of life, isn't it? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm whining about a few lost hours of sleep. I know it's not the worst thing in the world. That's fairly common for someone my age, wouldn't you say?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your appetite has been less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I haven't been quite as hungry as usual. I really don't want to make it sound like I'm starving myself or anything dramatic like that. I think maybe it's just because I'm not moving around as much as I did when I was working at the library, so I just don't need as much food, right?\n\nBut, well... I do sort of have to remind myself to eat these days. And my wife... she's so wonderful, she cooks these lovely meals, but she has noticed I've been leaving my rice and soup half-finished. I feel terrible about it, honestly. Before, I would never, ever waste a meal she made for me. I just... I just don't seem to have the room for it right now. \n\nThat's probably just a normal part of getting older and slowing down a bit, isn't it? I hope I'm not making her feel unappreciated... I really do try to eat what I can. That's not too concerning, is it?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings made it hard for you to manage your usual daily activities?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really wouldn't say it's completely ruined my days or anything. I mean, I still get up and get dressed, so I'm not totally useless, right? I try my best not to let things slip too much...\n\nBut, well... I suppose I have been a little bit disorganized lately. It's almost embarrassing to admit, really. I had all these grand plans for my retirement—putting together some lecture notes, organizing my travel journals, just... keeping busy, you know? And even just the simple household paperwork. But lately, they've just sort of... piled up on my desk. \n\nI just can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I used to when I was at the library. I used to be so good at keeping everything in its proper place. Now I just look at the stacks of paper and... I just don't know where to start. \n\nI feel awful about it, honestly. I don't want my wife to think I'm just being lazy now that I'm not working. I really am trying to get back on top of things. It's just a bit of a rough patch, wouldn't you say? I'm sure I'll get back to my old self soon enough, right?\n\nDoctor: Since retiring, have you found yourself feeling unable to settle into that change?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose the transition was a little bumpy at first. I really don't want to sound ungrateful for my retirement, because it's such a blessing to have this time, right? \n\nBut... right after I finished up at the library, there were some days where I felt almost... well, almost too bright and excited, if that makes sense. I just had so much nervous energy about all the things I could do. \n\nBut I feel just awful admitting this... I think I might have been a bit difficult to be around during those days. Sometimes I would get a little sharp with my family. Not yelling or anything terrible! Just... snapping over small things, like when they would gently suggest I slow down a bit. \n\nI feel terrible about it, honestly. They were just looking out for me, and I was just a bit too wound up. That's a pretty normal way to react to a big life change like retirement though, wouldn't you say? I really hope I don't sound like a grumpy old man... I'm usually very even-tempered, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Since you retired, have there been any days when your mood has felt normal again?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose things have been a bit settled into this... this quieter feeling for a little while now. I don't want to make it sound like I've been completely miserable every single day, because I really do try to keep a positive attitude about things, you know? \n\nBut, well... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't sound like I'm just complaining... it's been about three weeks now. Ever since that first sort of rush of nervous energy right after I retired faded away, I've just been in this... this slump, I guess you could call it. \n\nI haven't really had a day in these last three weeks where I felt quite like my usual, cheerful self. I feel terrible saying that out loud, like I'm not trying hard enough to enjoy my retirement. I really am trying to snap out of it. It's just taking a little longer than I expected to adjust, I suppose. That's not too unusual, is it? To need a few weeks to find your footing after working for so long?\n\nDoctor: During those days right after you retired when you felt unusually bright and excited, did you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose my sleep schedule did change a little bit during that time. I really hope it doesn't sound like I was doing anything unhealthy, because I actually felt quite good! \n\nBut, well... for several nights there, I think I was only sleeping maybe three or four hours. The strange thing was, I didn't feel tired at all. I would get up feeling completely refreshed and just... ready to work on all my new projects. It honestly felt like sleep was just getting in the way of everything I wanted to accomplish. \n\nI really wasn't tossing and turning or feeling miserable about it, I promise. That's just a normal burst of inspiration though, wouldn't you say? I've always had this little interest in Korean art from my time working at the library. Well, um... I guess I might have been a bit more talkative than usual. I really don't want to sound like I was being rude or dominating the conversation, because I always try to be a good listener. 

But, well... my daughter did mention something about it. She came over one afternoon, and I was just so excited to tell her all about my plans. I was going on about the Korean art lectures, and how we could coordinate with the local museums, and even figuring out the best train routes for people to come from out of town.

I think I was just so full of ideas and wanted to share them all at once. That happens sometimes when you're passionate about a new project, right? You just get a little too eager to share the details, wouldn't you say?
